9. Debt-funded revenue
10. DFRI publishes two linked records. It estimates the share of covered companies' U.S. consumer revenue funded by new consumer credit. It also predicts each month's change in U.S. consumer borrowing before the Federal Reserve publishes the official G.19 figure, records that forecast with a timestamp, and grades it against the first print.

23. Across the same publication, covered-company midpoint estimates range from 1.45% at Booking Holdings to 18.67% at Carvana. The 3.36% headline is revenue-weighted, not a threshold or a typical-company score.
24. Midpoint represents an estimated $19,792 million of debt-funded consumer revenue over an estimated $588,888 million denominator. Bands are always shown.

66. Every estimate separates directly observed financing links from category mappings and proportional allocation.
67. Tier definitions
68. T1 — OBSERVED
69. A company disclosure directly links financing to sales.
70. T2 — CATEGORY-MAPPED
71. Credit is mapped to spending categories, then to companies using registered weights.
72. T3 — FUNGIBLE
73. Credit that cannot be assigned directly is allocated broadly by estimated consumer revenue, with the widest uncertainty.
74. Tier percentages show how the estimate was constructed—not confidence scores. Full methodology

10. Each row is an immutable forecast of monthly U.S. consumer borrowing. Figures are seasonally adjusted flows in millions of U.S. dollars, always shown with 80% and 95% bands.
11. Predictions are recorded before the Federal Reserve publishes the official G.19 figure. Rows are newest first without JavaScript; browser controls add optional sorting. Actuals are release-coherent first prints, never later revisions.

27. The naive comparator is refit for each target using only first prints available when that prediction was recorded. These statistics update from live grades only and remain separate from the historical backtest.
28. First revolving miss
29. The prediction forecast -5,529 $M; the first print was +6,800 $M. It missed the sign and fell outside its 80% interval. Its absolute error was about 12,330 $M, versus a 4,633 $M 101-month backtest MAE.
30. One miss in one graded pair is statistically uninformative, but DFRI flags it rather than burying it. The model has not been retrained or retuned in response.

8. DATA SCHEMA
9. Methodology
10. The nowcast estimates monthly G.19 credit flows. Attribution then applies registered product-to-category weights (Matrix A) and category-to-company weights (Matrix B), divided by estimated U.S. consumer revenue. Every judgmental uncertain input has a public assumption ID and band; fixed derived mappings retain their method and evidence.
11. Primary nowcast sources: Federal Reserve Board H.8 release archive, Census MARTS historic releases, and the Federal Reserve Board G.19 release archive used for first-print grading.
12. Read the complete code-derived modeling architecture, including implemented benchmarks, selection rules, point-in-time controls, attribution mechanics, and documented divergences.
13. Methodology 1.1.1 preserves the 50-company coverage boundary and corrects Carvana's auto-market and originated-finance classification. Compare 1.1.0 with 1.1.1 or inspect the dated coverage and exclusion ledger.
14. Bridge model
15. Partial H.8 months expose observed coverage explicitly. Missing retail data remains missing and is represented by an explicit availability indicator whose coefficient is fitted with the bridge.
16. First-print grading
17. The dated release page supplies the target-month preliminary level and revised prior-month level used for the release-coherent flow.
18. Ledger
19. Predictions and grades are separate. Model upgrades create new versions; old rows are neither restated nor deleted.
20. Tier 1
21. Observed: a disclosure directly links financing to the company's sales. The company page links the filing and a quote of no more than 15 words.
22. Tier 2
23. Category-mapped: credit flow is allocated to spend categories and then to companies by estimated category revenue share.
24. Tier 3
25. Fungible: unassignable flows are allocated in proportion to covered consumer revenue, with the widest bands.
26. Credit flow
27. The view uses the registered current-period midpoint flow, Matrix A midpoint, and Matrix B midpoint for each lane. It groups only after those published mappings are applied, keeps every remainder group within one evidence tier, and does not enter the nowcast or change any company estimate.
28. 2026-Q1 · registered midpoint allocations · millions of U.S. dollars
29. Width = estimated dollars
30. Style = how much is actually known

64. Evidence Lift
65. Evidence Lift equals a company's estimated DFR% midpoint divided by its pure Tier 3 fungibility baseline for the same period. The baseline is a counterfactual built from the already-computed broad proportional lanes—general retail plus the two fungible categories—with company-specific financing and auto-category evidence removed.
66. A value of 1.00× is baseline-only: no company-specific financing evidence was found, so the estimate reflects proportional allocation. A higher multiple shows how much registered evidence lifts the midpoint above that baseline. It does not measure risk, credit quality, or investment merit.
67. Backtest coverage
68. In the point-in-time backtest, the nominal 80% band contained 71.3% of first prints and the nominal 95% band contained 93.1%. Both currently under-cover their nominal rates, especially the 80% interval, so these bands are empirical uncertainty ranges, not guarantees.
69. Assumptions
70. Low / midpoint / high values are triangular priors unless all three values are identical. Source files are public so a cold clone can reproduce every result.
71. Every non-observed attribution parameter in methodology 1.1.1.

221. A-T1-CVNA-FINANCE-001 TIER 1 Carvana receives 1% to 3% of national nonrevolving flow through its originated used-auto finance channel. 0.01 / 0.02 / 0.03 Source
222. Since February 2013, Carvana has also offered and originated loans to consumers. The wide prior is scaled below GM and Ford using Carvana's smaller registered consumer-revenue denominator; the filing and ABS trust establish origination.
223. Matrix A and B
224. Matrix A has 15 product-to-category rows. Matrix B has 165 category-to-company rows. Tests reject negative weights, missing evidence, unregistered uncertainty, or row allocations over 100%.

9. Coverage
10. The P1 universe partitions every Consumer Discretionary and Consumer Staples member in the pinned, cross-checked S&P 500 snapshot. Historical coverage is preserved by methodology version; no past company set is rewritten.
11. Verified 2026-08-05. Fifty included, 31 excluded, with a one-line reason for every exclusion. Retain the ten P0 issuers, then rank passing issuers by direct consumer-revenue evidence, geographic evidence, filing completeness, and ticker as the deterministic tie-break. No price, market-cap, or vendor data is used.

11. 2026-08-09 · publication · 1.1.1
12. Company directory and ledger path clarified
13. Separated the homepage claims into an ordered reading path, promoted the immutable prediction ledger, grouped 38 indistinguishable baseline-only Evidence Lift rows behind a native disclosure, and added a complete alphabetical company directory without changing any estimate or feed.
14. Companies · Prediction ledger · Evidence Lift
15. 2026-08-08 · publication · 1.1.1
16. Numbers promoted; credit flow added
17. Compressed the heading scale so figures carry the hierarchy and added a server-rendered current-period flow view whose ribbon width represents estimated dollars and whose solid, dashed, and dotted styles expose evidence tiers without color dependence.
18. Credit flow · Methodology
19. 2026-08-08 · publication · bridge-ridge-v2-alpha10
20. First live grades published without softening
21. Published the first two live grades, prominently flagging that the revolving prediction missed direction and its 80% interval; added running live-only coverage and MAE comparisons without retraining or retuning the model.
22. Scoreboard · Scoreboard feed
23. 2026-08-08 · publication · 1.1.1
24. Graded feed status corrected
25. Corrected the scoreboard compatibility status so every graded row reports GRADED, matching grade\_status; the feed schema now states and tests that invariant.
26. Scoreboard feed · Feed schema
27. 2026-08-06 · methodology · 1.1.1
28. Carvana auto-finance classification corrected
29. Added Carvana's SEC-filed originated-auto-finance and ABS trust evidence, included CVNA in the covered-auto allocation, and published the derived Evidence Lift ranking under a non-breaking v2 company-feed schema.
30. Evidence Lift · Carvana · Company feed v2 · Feed schema v2
31. 2026-08-05 · publication · 1.1.0
32. Quarterly attribution refresh demonstrated
33. Recomputed the 2026-Q1 50-company attribution from six Board first-print monthly flows and point-in-time SEC filing facts; 35 denominators advanced through comparable 10-Q facts and 15 remained explicitly on their annual baseline.
34. Quarterly refresh feed · Company history feed · Methodology
35. 2026-08-05 · methodology · 1.1.0
36. P1 coverage expanded to 50 companies

38. Coverage ledger · Version comparison · Company feed

42. Scoreboard · Methodology
